New pure helpers: window_plan, clamp_window_rect(rect, screens, primary, place, preferred), capture_window, ui_factor, effective_pct (reports 178 for 200 % at 1280x720), audio_values, apply_ui, apply_frame_limits.
- AppApply has three injectable static sinks so later modules plug in without app edits: audio_sink(values: Dictionary) for the [audio]+announcer_tts values (AppAudio/Snd, UI-05), quality_sink(cfg: ConfigFile) for UiViewPort.apply_quality (the view), input_sink(id, store) for UiKeymap and camera settings (UI-09a). AppApply.quality_config leaves the window keys (window_mode, resolution, window_pos, monitor, ui_scale, vsync, background_fps) out of [video], and 'cvd' is written as colour_mode=deutan plus cvd_palette=true.
- AppBoot is instance-based: AppBoot.run(host) builds an instance (AppBoot.last) and calls execute(host, args), so tests can drive it. It exposes timeline(), over_budget(factor), splash_ms, unclean, finished and fatal. Soft-linked services, each probed by class name with UiDraw.optional_script and called if the static method exists: ViewStyle.load_file(), UiText.load_all(), NetReplay.recover_orphans(), UiKeymap.load_from(store), UiCursors.register_all(), UiIconBaker.prewarm() (skipped headless, skipped with --no-prewarm), the autostart harness AppTestBoot.run(host, args), and the hook AppBootHook.run(state, store, args). UI-05 should put AudioApp.setup, AppNet wiring and the audio_sink registration into AppBootHook.run.
- AppLaunchArgs adds fields no_prewarm, with_ui, roster, ui_scale (float, 0 = unset), palette, renderer_relaunched, timeout_s, shot, positional, plus manages_window() and is_test_mode(). Test modes (smoke, selfcheck, shot, --qa, --autostart, --fresh-settings) skip the .session sentinel and the renderer relaunch. --shot=... also stops saved settings from resizing the window, so tools/gd shot keeps its --size.
- AppScenes: goto() coalesces requests queued during a transition (the one in flight always completes), and the signal transition_finished fires when the queue drains. toast() returns the UiToast. Extra API: ensure_built(), navigate_requested(target, params) and back_requested() signals, top_screen(), pushed_count(), fade_alpha(), is_busy(), set_fps_visible(on). Toast actions accept {label_key, label?, call}. AppState maps navigate(target) to flow modes (target &"quit" quits); AppFlow gains mode_of_screen(id, params), back_of(mode), mode_name(m), is_overlay(m), force(mode, params).
- AppCrash.build(reason, detail) returns the model {reason, name, message, detail, text, retry} for the fatal screen, and AppCrash.show() returns it. AppCrash.reporter and AppCrash.settings_digest are static hooks set by AppBoot. AppCrashReporter is configurable (files, session_path, crash_dir, logs_dir, pid_provider, info_provider, phase, user_path_override); info_provider returns {rosters, map_family, map_size, map_seed, tick, checksum, net_role} (only these keys are printed, addresses redacted). toast_actions(prev, watch_replay) builds the unclean-exit toast actions. AppState sets crash_reporter.phase on every mode change; the session is ended in AppState._exit_tree, so a kill -9 or crash leaves the sentinel behind.
- AppLogSink.emit takes tag as String (not StringName). AppLogger writes Log.ring directly under its own mutex (Log itself is not thread-safe), records the first error and every 100th of an identical one, and emits error_logged deferred. AppLogSink/AppLogger are held in static vars of AppBoot; boot.gd _exit_tree calls AppBoot.shutdown_logging(). A screenshot taken while boot is still mid-splash quits with a leaked-resources engine error, so use --screen=splash to hold the splash.
- AppSplash (class in app_splash.gd) and AppPlaceholderScreen (app_placeholder_screen.gd) are extra files not in the task list. AppScreens.make(id) prefers a real class UiScreen<PascalCase(id)> (UiScreenMainMenu, UiScreenLobby, UiScreenLanBrowser, ...), so UI-06a screens plug in with no edit here. Screen ids: splash, main_menu, lobby (params.lan for the LAN lobby), lan_browser, loading, game (params.replay), end, replays, field_manual, options, credits, fatal. AppSplash exposes set_status(text) and set_progress(0..1); AppBoot only calls them if the screen has them.
- project.godot: added the AppSettings, AppState, AppScenes autoloads after DevShot; display/window/stretch/mode is now "disabled" (UI1 request, the factor is the only scaling) and display/window/dpi/allow_hidpi=true; debug/settings/crash_handler/message set. AppNet is not registered (UI-05 adds it, before AppState per 2.1).

**Tests:** `tools/gd check --strict src/app tests/ui`: 0 script errors, 0 lint violations, 0 warnings. A whole-project `tools/gd check --strict` fails only in other agents' files (view_selection.gd L006 prints, tests/scratch, tests/support, tests/visual/vw_battle.gd). `tools/gd test test_ui_boot test_ui_crash test_ui_apply test_ui_logger test_ui_flow test_ui_launch test_ui_settings -q`: 53 tests passed, 954 checks. The same filters under `tools/gd linux test`: 53 passed, 954 checks. Per file: test_ui_settings 14 (includes test_app_settings_atomic_write, test_app_window_clamp, debounce/override), test_ui_apply 10, test_ui_logger 10 (worker-thread and two-thread tests, real push_error), test_ui_crash_reporter 6 (includes test_app_crash_report_fields), test_ui_flow 5 (includes AppState over AppScenes), test_ui_launch_args 4, test_ui_boot 4 (timeline, scenes, registry, smoke contract). `tools/gd run res://src/app/boot.tscn -- --smoke` printed `MERIDIAN_BOOT engine=4.7.2-stable renderer=forward_plus os=macOS debug=1 version=0.1.0 build=dev selftest=ok` and exited 0. Screenshots: `tools/gd shot res://src/app/boot.tscn out.png --size 1920x1080 --frames 60 -- --screen=splash --progress=0.62 --status=

## UI3A:ports+selection+commands

**Summary:** UI-04a, UI-09b and UI-09c are done. All 131 new tests pass, and `tools/gd check --strict` is clean for my files. The full-project strict check still fails on files that are not mine (print calls in `src/view/overlay/view_selection.gd`, warnings in `tests/scratch` and `tests/visual`). Commands go through `UiCommandBus` and `UiCmdCodec` (the `SimCmd` builders) to a net port. `UiNetPortLoopback` feeds a real `SimWorld`, so the game can run single-player without sockets. `UiSimPortWorld` is a real adapter over `SimWorld`, with fog integrity (an unseen enemy cannot be read live) and vision ghosts. `UiSimPortFixture` plus `tests/fixtures/ui/hud_mid_match.json` give fixture-driven tests and screenshots on real `GameData`. The resolver has 113 rule and armed-mode cases, the codec reproduces all golden commands of 6.1, and the world test proves the move, attack, produce and place effects on a real `SimWorld`.

**Deviations (downstream code relies on these):**
- `UiSelection.settle()` is added and `prune` calls it. `on_removed` swaps an MCV and its Headquarters in place and only sets the mode when every id is one kind, because several MCVs of one batch swap one after another. A mixed result waits for `settle()` (units win over structures).
- `UiSelection.successors_of(records, out, parent_of = Callable())` (static) pairs REMOVED(DEPLOYED) with SPAWNED(DEPLOYED): by `parent_of(spawned) == removed` first, then by emission order per owner. `sel.def_of(eid)` and `count_of_def` exist.
- The `kind` of `UiCommandBus.issued` and `refused` is the SimCmd op of the command (0 for a resolver DENIED). It is not the UiOrderIntent kind.
- Extra bus helpers: `train_cancel_def(producer, unit_def)` cancels the last instance of that def; `ping(sim_x, sim_y)` sends the map ping; `turn_count()`; `rally_fallback: Callable` (returns the active tab's producers, used when SET_RALLY has no selected producer); `move_speed_match` and `move_reverse` bools fill the MOVE flags. `hold(ids)` and `follow(...)` exist as in the spec. Identical consecutive commands within a turn are dropped silently (no `refused`).
- `UiCmdCodec.load_units(...)` is the LOAD builder (a static named `load` would clash with the GDScript builtin). It also has `cell_center(c)`, `angle_of(dx, dy)` and `norm_to_sim`. Its builders take `queued: bool` and add QM_APPEND only on ops the catalog flags M_QUEUE. `attack(ids, target, queued, forced)`, `harvest(ids, x, y, queued)` (sim units, target 0), `unload(ids, all, passenger, x, y)`.
- Resolver mods are `UiContextResolver.MOD_QUEUE = 1` and `MOD_FORCE = 2` (the roles after `UiKeymap.mods_of`, equal to SHIFT and CTRL). Armed SELL and REPAIR resolve without a unit selection and put the clicked structure id in `intent.ids`. Armed RALLY with no producer selected returns SET_RALLY with empty ids, and the bus asks `rally_fallback`. Armed PING, POWER, SUPERWEAPON, PLACE and ABILITY resolve to NONE (input controller, `UiTargeting` and `UiPlacement` handle them). WAYPOINT armed means queue everything. `UiModes.effective_mods(mods)` ORs in the latch.
- `UiTarget` gains `caps`, `repairing` and `selling`; `UiOrderIntent` gains `def_idx` and the `CUR_*` and `MK_*` constants (cursor and marker integers equal `UiCursors.State` and the marker enum, because `UiCursors` does not exist yet). `UiEntityRow` gains `capturable` and `garrison_free` (filled by the adapters for neutral structures). `UiSelectionInfo` gains `ids`, `caps`, `defs`, `move_mask`, `move_classes`, `index_of`, `caps_of`, `def_of`, and the pure builder `from_entries(mode, ids, caps, defs, move_classes)`.
- Construction: `UiSimPortWorld` reports `Construction.BUILDING` for every non-empty, non-ready state, including paused and held ones. The reason is in `queue_state` (HELD, WAIT_FUNDS, PREREQ_LOST, LOW_POWER ...). `Construction.PAUSED` is never produced.
- The fixture keeps progress in millionths internally (`advance(n)` re-derives progress, eta, cooldown and charge linearly). Its extra API: `blank()`, `add_player`, `spawn`, `add_entity`, `remove_entity`, `hide_entity`, `force_rule`, `set_visibility`, `set_blocked`, `set_deposit`, `set_bad_site`, `set_sell_value`, `set_producer`, `set_construction`, `set_power`, `set_superweapon`, `push_event`, `row_of`, `shared_data()`. `read()` of an unseen enemy is false; a ghost reads as an F_GHOST row.
- `UiSimPortFixture.load_file(name)` resolves names under `FIXTURE_DIR = "tests/fixtures/ui"` (a constant, because lint rule L005 forbids `res://tests/` literals in `src`); a full res:// path also works.
- `UiSimPortWorld(world, local_pid)`: `read()` of an enemy or neutral entity the viewer cannot see returns the remembered `SimGhost` row (F_GHOST) or false. `bind_alpha(callable)`, `set_alpha`, `sim_world()`, and static `rule_of_rsn(rsn)`, `place_reason_of(rsn)`, `queue_state_of(qs, power_short)` are public. Placement RSN reasons other than OK map to BAD_SITE with `place_reason()` 1-5.
- New `UiNetPortLoopback(world, pid)` (`ui_net_port_loopback.gd`): forwards to `SimWorld.submit_raw`, executed by the next `step()`. `UiNetPortSession` and `UiAudioPortSnd` call `NetSession` and `Snd` by name at call time (Object.call), so they compile without those modules.
- Duck-typed collaborators, because their classes do not exist yet: `UiSelectionActions.setup(sim, view, selection, notifier)`, `UiCommandBus.setup(net, sim, view, audio, feedback)`, `UiFeedback.setup(overlay, minimap, audio, notifier)` and `UiContextResolver.make_target(port, view, ...)` take Objects. They call `entity_screen_rect`, `focus_on_sim`, `camera_state`, `pick`, `pick_ground`, `world_to_sim`, `add_marker(kind, x, y, target)`, `add_ping(kind, x, y)` and `notice(key)` by name. `UiFeedback` also exposes every effect as a signal (`marker_requested`, `ping_requested`, `flash_requested`, `denied_shown`), and `UiSelectionActions` has `nothing_found`.
- `UiModes` does not call `UiCursors` and takes an optional `audio` port. The screen wires `changed` to the cursor. `UiUnitCaps.shared_for(port)` is a process-wide cache rebuilt when data or the viewer's roster changes.

**Tests:** ["`tools/gd check game/src/ui game/tests/ui --strict` -> RESULT: OK (0 script errors, 0 lint violations, 0 warnings).", "`tools/gd test ui/test_ui_fixture_port ui/test_ui_ev ui/test_ui_selection ui/test_ui_selection_actions ui/test_ui_context_resolver ui/test_ui_cmd_codec ui/test_ui_command_bus ui/test_ui_world_commands ui/test_ui_modes -q` -> 131 tests, 131 passed, 0 failed (1525 checks).", "`tools/gd test ui -q` (whole ui folder, including other agents' tests) -> 248 passed, 0 failed.", "`tools/gd linux test ui/test_ui_ -q` -> 228 passed, 0 failed.", "Test coverage: fixture and world adapters against one shared contract (`contract_ui_sim_port.gd`, also with fog on); UiEv against the sim constants (`verify_against_sim()` returns []); selection survival and the successor rule; 113 resolver calls; every golden of 6.1; command bus budget, duplicate, observer, fog, prune and extras cases; UI commands executed by a real SimWorld (move, queued move, attack, forced-attack refusal, train and refund, build then place, sell, death hook).", "A leak notice (ObjectDB instances / resources still in use) prints at exit for any test that loads `GameData` (the shared static in `UiSimPortFixture`).

**Known gaps:**
- `UiSimPortWorld` degrades to neutral values where the sim domain has not landed. `strategic_warnings()` is empty until `SimStrategicSystem.warnings_affecting` exists. `UiEntityRow.cargo`, `squad` and `vet` stay at their defaults because `SimCompCargo` is still a stub; `cargo_cap` comes from the transport ability param. `repair_cost_per_second` returns 0 (the repair loop is not in the sim). `power_*` and `sw_*` read the economy slot records and are untested against a live sim; the fixture covers them.
- `UiTarget.transport_vehicle_free` is approximated (the def's capacity_vehicles_n while nothing is loaded), and `salvageable` is approximated as a wreck of a non-friendly land vehicle (no wreck-window data in the row).
- `UiSelectionActions` has no control-group cycling (UiControlGroups is UI-09a) and reports 'nothing found' as a signal or a duck-typed `notice(key)` call, since `UiNotifier` does not exist yet.
- `UiViewPort` (UI-04b), `UiCursors`, `UiText`, `UiOrderMarkers`, `UiMinimap` and `UiNotifier` are not in this task. The code that names them uses duck typing or integer constants.
- Not tested: `UiNetPortSession` against a real `NetSession`, and `UiAudioPortSnd` against a real `Snd` autoload. Tested only with a fake session and the silent no-op path.
- Two tests loop on the `Rig` helper class with lambdas that capture it (reference cycles), so the exit shows a leak notice; harmless.

**Cross-module requests:**
- View (UI-04b `UiViewPort`): must implement `pick(screen, filter)`, `pick_ground(screen)` (Vector3, INF on miss), `world_to_sim(Vector3) -> Vector2i`, `entity_screen_rect(eid) -> Rect2`, `focus_on_sim(x, y, instant)` and `camera_state()` (`{focus_x, focus_z}` in world metres). The bus, actions and `make_target` call them by name.
- Sim / abilities: fill `SimCompCargo` with a loaded-squad count (and a transport-vehicle count) so `UiSimPortWorld.read` can set `cargo` and `transport_vehicle_free`. Also expose infantry squad members and veterancy on entities for `squad`, `squad_max` and `vet`.
- Sim / economy: provide `SimStrategicSystem.warnings_affecting(pid, out: Array)` (fills `SimWarning`) and a `repair_cost_per_second`-style read helper, per [XR-2..4]. `UiSimPortWorld` picks the first up by `has_method` with no further change.
- App (UI-03b / UI-05 / UI-15): bind `UiNetPortLoopback(world, local_pid)` as the net port for skirmish and `UiSimPortWorld(world, local_pid)` as the sim port at match start. Call `UiSelection.prune(port)` and `UiSelection.settle()` after each tick batch and `UiSelection.successors_of(records, out, parent_of)` plus `on_removed` per drained event batch. Call `UiCommandBus.begin_turn_budget(tick)` or let the bus follow `net.tick() >> 1`.
- UI-10 / UI-11: `UiOrderMarkers.add_marker(kind, x, y, target)`, `UiMinimap.add_ping(kind, x, y)` and `UiNotifier.notice(key)` are the names `UiFeedback` and `UiSelectionActions` call (or connect the `UiFeedback` signals instead).
- UI-01b: `UiCursors.State` integers must equal the `UiOrderIntent.CUR_*` constants (0 DEFAULT, 3 ATTACK, 4 MOVE, 5 SELECT, 6 DENIED, 7 ATTACK_MOVE, 8 FORCE_FIRE, 9 GUARD, 11 SELL, 12 REPAIR, 13 RALLY, 14 INSPECT, 16 INTERACT), and the text keys `order.deny.*`, `reject.<n>`, `notice.unit_cap` and `notice.select.*` need entries in `ui_en.json`.
- project.godot: no change needed from this task.

## UI3B:input+keymap+picking

**Summary:** UI-09a and UI-04b are complete and pass their tests. The input controller, actions, keymap, control groups, bookmarks and `data/ui/keymap_defaults.json` were already in place and passing when I started. The fixture view port, `UiPickData` and `UiPicking` were also there. This session I added the real adapter `UiViewPortWorld` over `ViewWorld`, since `src/view/view_picker.gd` now exists. I also fixed the fixture battlefield, which rendered no terrain at all. Its triangle winding was back-facing, so the ground was culled and the screen showed only the clear colour. I retuned lighting, ground colours and stand-in colours over four screenshot iterations, but the stand-in structures and tanks still look washed out and pinkish. A new lab scene `lab_input.tscn` shows the overlay/picking alignment with a drag rectangle and selection brackets. Picking timing at 439 entities was ray 57 us and box 109 us, against the spike's 127 / 92 us.

**Deviations (downstream code relies on these):**
- `UiViewPortWorld.new(world: ViewWorld)` is the production adapter. It sets `ViewCamera.auto_input = false` and `edge_scroll_enabled = false`, and it sits in `ui_view_port_world.gd`, the one UI file that names `View*` classes.
- `UiViewPortWorld.poll_camera()` is new and not in the spec. The screen should call it once per frame after `ViewWorld.frame`. It emits `camera_changed` at most every 33 ms, and `ViewCamera.view_changed` also triggers it.
- `UiViewPortWorld.set_camera_state(s, false)` writes the rig's private smoothing targets (`_yaw_t`, `_zoom_t`, `_bias_t`) directly. `ViewCamera` has no public smooth set-all call. I suggest the view owner add one. With `instant = true` it uses the public `snap_to`.
- `camera_state()` returns `{focus_x, focus_z, yaw, zoom, pitch_bias}` in metres, degrees, 0..1 and degrees. The fixture adapter uses the same keys, but its `zoom` is a camera distance in metres, not 0..1. Bookmarks saved with one adapter must not be restored with the other.
- `UiViewPortWorld` does not implement range rings, rally/order lines, the placement ghost, float text, the minimap texture, icons, models, showcase, map preview or quality, because those `ViewWorld` subsystems do not exist yet. Those members keep the `UiViewPort` no-op defaults.
- Bug fix in `UiFixtureBattlefield`: the terrain triangle winding was back-facing, so the ground never rendered. `UiHudPresenter`/`lab_hud` screenshots showed an empty grey ground before this fix.
- `UiViewPortFixture` stand-in colours are darker and more neutral. Only the public API of `UiViewPortFixture` matters downstream, and it is unchanged.

**Tests:** `tools/gd check --strict` reports 0 script errors and 0 lint violations. Its remaining warnings are in other agents' files (tests/scenarios, tests/scratch, tests/visual/lab_hud.gd). Test runs, all through `tools/gd test <filter>`:
- ui_keymap: 8 tests, 566 checks, pass.
- ui_groups: 5 tests, 32 checks, pass.
- ui_edge: 2 tests, 20 checks, pass.
- ui_picking: 7 tests, 47 checks, pass. Timing note: "UiPicking at 439 entities: ray_pick 57 us, box_select 109 us; spike reference 127 / 92 us".
- ui_lab_input (cases A-S, both lab files): 22 tests, 120 checks, pass.
- ui_input_script: 4 tests, 55 checks, pass. It covers box-select, right-click move, right-click attack, shift-click toggle, control-group recall and armed attack-move, all producing the right commands on a real SimWorld through the bus.
- ui_view_port_world (new): 3 tests, 20 checks, pass.
- `tools/gd test ui -q`: 322 tests, 321 passed, 1 error. The error is `test_ui_boot::test_boot_timeline_within_budgets`. It is not from my files: `app_boot.gd:288` fails with "Can't call non-static function 'load_from'", and the data-load step took 352 ms against a 270 ms budget.
- Screenshots: `tools/gd shot res://tests/visual/lab_input.tsc

**Known gaps:**
- Only the camera, picking, selection, hover, health-bar mode and team-colour members of `UiViewPortWorld` are exercised, and only headless on a flat terrain fixture. Nothing in the new adapter has been run in a live game screen.
- In `test_ui_view_port_world`, `UiPicking` (the fixture sphere picker) is not compared against `ViewPicker`, because the test camera is not in the scene tree. The two are only checked separately.
- `UiViewPortWorld.frustum_ground_quad` falls back to a far point along the ray when a corner misses the ground, and I did not test that path.
- Sim-driven control-group recall was tested, but I did not test the real double-tap focus feel or any glyph or cursor feedback.
- The fixture battlefield is still a dev stand-in: boxes and capsules on noise terrain, with no decor or models.
- `test_ui_input_script.gd`, `test_ui_lab_input2.gd` and `ui_input_glue.gd` have no `.uid` files yet. Godot generates them on the next import, which is harmless.

**Cross-module requests:**
- Task UI-15: wire `UiViewPortWorld.new(view_world)` in `AppMatch.make_context`, call `poll_camera()` after `ViewWorld.frame`, and set `ViewWorld.drive_camera = true` so the rig's `advance` still smooths.
- View owner: add public API to `ViewCamera` for (a) smooth-setting all pose targets (focus, yaw, zoom, bias) at once and (b) reading the pitch bias. `UiViewPortWorld` currently reaches into private members for the smooth restore.
- View owner: add `ViewWorld` subsystems with these members, then wire them in `UiViewPortWorld`: range rings, rally/order lines, placement ghost, float text, minimap source, icon bake.
- App owner: `AppBoot` line 288 calls a non-static `load_from`, which breaks `test_ui_boot::test_boot_timeline_within_budgets`.

## UI4:hud+minimap

**Summary:** UI4 (UI-08a, UI-08b, UI-10 essentials) is done. The full in-game HUD runs on a real SimWorld (SimMatchKit) through UiSimPortWorld, the loopback net port and the fixture view. It covers sidebar tabs and cards in every state, credits, power bar, queue strip, power dock, minimap with feed and fog placeholder, selection panel, command and ability bars, group badges, ribbons and alerts, placement and targeting. Most source files were already present from an earlier pass. This pass added the rig, tests and labs, fixed several bugs, and iterated visually at 1920x1080 and 1280x720. All my tests pass and my files have no strict warnings. The full ui suite has one error, in test_ui_boot (app_boot.gd:288, load_from is not static), which is not my code.

**Deviations (downstream code relies on these):**
- UiHudPresenter.add_marker(marker_kind, sim_x, sim_y, target_eid = -1) and add_ping(kind, sim_x, sim_y) were added so the presenter can be passed as both the overlay and the minimap of UiFeedback.setup(presenter, presenter, audio, notifier). UiFeedback calls those two method names, but UiWorldOverlay.add_order_marker and UiMinimap.add_ping(norm, col, kind) have different signatures.
- UiHudPresenter.setup_ports now calls hud.overlay().setup(view, sim) itself, so the screen does not need to.
- The presenter's refresh_all() now resets its cadence counters (full / feed / slow) to 0 after refreshing, so the first on_ticks does not refresh a second time. set_modes() and _wire() no longer double-connect UiModes.changed. The presenter now listens to UiControlGroups.changed to refresh the badges. A newly selected producer takes over the queue strip.
- Queue strip title for structures is now 'BUILD  //  QUEUE' (was 'CONSTRUCTION  //  QUEUE', which clipped in UiQueueStrip). The sidebar footer 'BUILD RADIUS' is now read from the roster HQ's build_radius / 1024 instead of the literal 8.
- UiHud.RIBBON_W = 560 (spec metric 480). 'ENEMY SUPERWEAPON DETECTED' plus the countdown clipped in UiRibbon at 480.
- The minimap fog placeholder texture is skipped only when view.minimap_material() is a ShaderMaterial. The fixture view returns a plain CanvasItemMaterial, so the placeholder now shows there.
- Test rig for downstream tests and labs: tests/ui/ui_hud_rig.gd, preload it as _Rig. build(host, {"fixture": UiSimPortFixture} or {seed, size, warm, rosters, rules, faction, render}); pump(ticks); card(name); click_card(name, count); base_center(); focus_base(). Members: world, sim, view, net, audio, selection, groups, bus, placement, targeting, feedback, presenter, hud, modes.

**Tests:** tools/gd test test_ui_build_model: 9 passed (187 checks). tools/gd test test_ui_hud: 13 passed (118 checks). tools/gd test ui: 335 tests, 334 passed, 1 error (test_ui_boot::test_boot_timeline_within_budgets, app_boot.gd:288, not my code). tools/gd check --strict: 0 lint violations, 0 script errors; the 4 remaining warnings are in tests/scenarios/soak_rosters.gd and soak_units.gd, none in my files. Screenshots viewed (saved in the scratchpad dir): mid, select, units, lowpower, placement, targeting, factories, the hud_mid_match fixture, sw (enemy superweapon warning), fog. All at 1920x1080 and 1280x720. Visual iterations fixed: queue title clipping, ribbon title clipping, group badges not filling, minimap fog placeholder suppressed by the fixture's material. Lab command: tools/gd shot res://tests/visual/lab_hud.tscn out.png --size 1920x1080 -- --scenario=mid|select|units|factories|lowpower|placement|targeting [--fog=1] [--tab=N] [--ui-scale=x] or -- --fixture=hud_mid_match --scenario=mid|vehicles|sw.

**Known gaps:**
- No model portraits: cards and the selection portrait use the glyph placeholders until the icon service lands. icon_provider is wired but unused.
- Notifier integration is only the basic alerts: base under attack, collector and unit attack, structure ready, low power, superweapon ready and warning ribbons, unit-lost batching. It is used when no notifier with on_event is injected.
- UiOrderMarkers, UiSelectMenu and the strategic-zoom markers (V-hud-strategic) are not implemented; ui_order_markers and ui_select_menu are outside this task's essentials list. V-hud-observer is not implemented, so the observer HUD mode has no UI. Sidebar-left mirroring is not implemented.
- The bench gates (ui_bench overlay <= 0.05 ms, strategic markers <= 0.5 ms) were not measured.
- In the real sim, sw_status() stays NONE for a superweapon launcher spawned directly with spawn_structure. Only the economy build path sets it, so the F8 card shows 'Needs Atlas Kine...' in the lab's spawn-all scenarios.
- The placement ghost and its reason text are drawn by the fixture overlay. The real ghost belongs to VIEW-O2. UiPlacement.state is a ViewPlacementState filled from SimPlacementResult and is the data feed.
- Card, power and clock-sweep numbers overlay the icon centre (as in the prototype); no further visual polish beyond the two iterations.

**Cross-module requests:**
- UI5 screen glue: call feedback.setup(presenter, presenter, audio, notifier), and route world gestures through presenter.world_motion(screen), world_press(screen, button), world_release(screen, button) and cancel_modes() before the normal input handling. Feed presenter.on_events(records) from the AppEvents fan-out (order 20), on_ticks(n) after each step batch and on_frame(delta) per frame.
- UI1 (UiQueueStrip / UiRibbon): a title above about 190 px is clipped at the sidebar width, and the ribbon title width is size.x - 67 - 160 - 14, so 'ENEMY SUPERWEAPON DETECTED' needs a ribbon at least 540 px wide or a smaller right_w for countdown ribbons.
- UI-app (AppBoot): tests/ui/test_ui_boot.gd fails with "Can't call non-static function 'load_from'" at app_boot.gd:288.

## UI5:menus+lobby+match-glue

**Summary:** UI5 is done: the game now runs end to end in single-player, from main menu through lobby, loading and a live match to leave, with the HUD, terrain and units on screen and every screen inspected at 1920x1080 and 1280x720.
- Match glue: a local loopback session advances the sim at game speed and exposes the same surface the future NetSession will. AI slots run through an injectable hook that is a no-op until the AI module exists. `--bots` plays AI slots with the tests/support SimBot; `--bots=all` also plays the human slot, for a watchable match.
- Loading builds the map on a worker thread, then the world, then the view. The view module does not compose atmosphere, water, decor, fog or the minimap yet, so I added `AppViewStage` to do it. The game screen assembles view, HUD, presenter, input controller and command bus on the real ports.
- Menus: main menu with a real-view 3D showcase (arid-dusk orbit camera, featured faction rotating every 30 s), skirmish lobby (8 slots, faction and subfaction pickers backed by the bible, map preview, rules, briefing, all 5.14.4 start errors), loading screen with map preview, tips and per-player bars, credits, fatal, message and confirm dialogs.
- Determinism holds: the same config gives identical checksums across runs, and the 15 match tests also pass in the Linux container.
- Not done: the first-run dialog, LAN roles in the lobby, and chat. The LAN, Replays and Options menu buttons are disabled until those screens exist.
- Visible limits from other modules: structures render as magenta boxes, build cards fall back to glyphs (no icon baking yet), and there is no placement ghost.

**Deviations (downstream code relies on these):**
- No NetSession/NetWorldJob/AppNet/AppEvents/AppAudio exist, so `AppLocalSession` mirrors the NetSession surface: Role/Phase enums, `poll()`, `tick_alpha()`, `world()`, `config()`, `submit_command()`, `surrender()`, `request_pause()`, `host_set_speed()`, `leave()`, `shutdown()`, signals phase_changed, load_progress, launch_aborted, match_started, match_ended and pause_changed. It is created with `AppLocalSession.local_from_config(config, builder)`; the extra `job()` accessor lets the loading screen read progress.
- `AppMatchJob` is a plain RefCounted with the NetWorldJob shape (`step(budget_us)`, `progress_pct()`, `error()`, `phase_name()`) but returns `take_world() -> SimWorld` and `take_stage()`, not a NetSimAdapter. Phases are MAP, WORLD, VIEW, DONE (0-45, 45-50, 50-99, 100 percent), not the spec's SIM/VIEW/AUDIO.
- `AppMatch.start_local(config, opts) -> AppMatchContext` is the single launch call. It registers the context in `AppState.match_ctx`, adds an `AppSessionDriver` node that polls the session each frame (priority -100), and binds the flow itself in `AppMatch._bind_flow` (match_started -> IN_MATCH with {ctx}; launch_aborted -> SKIRMISH_LOBBY with {error}) instead of `AppState.bind_session`. `bind_session` would jump straight to END_SCREEN on match_ended, and the game screen needs to show its end banner first. It then navigates to `end` with `{summary}` (`AppMatch.result_summary`).
- `AppMatchContext.build_ports(stage)` runs when the world is ready. `net` is `AppNetPortLocal`, not `UiNetPortSession`, because `UiNetPortSession` calls `local_pid()` as a method while NetSession exposes it as a variable. The context also has `stage`, `driver`, `title` and `preview` (debug tags for the game screen).
- `AppViewStage` (a Node3D) composes ViewWorld with ViewMoodDef, ViewAtmosphere, ViewWater, ViewDecor, ViewFogOfWar and ViewMinimapSource, because ViewWorld does not compose them. It is parented under `AppScenes.backdrop_host()`. `AppViewPort extends UiViewPortWorld` adds `minimap_texture()`, `minimap_material()` and `bake_map_preview()`. `AppShowcase` builds the menu backdrop synchronously in one frame after a threaded map job.
- In headless runs `with_view=false`: no stage, and the context's view is a `UiViewPortFixture(sim, 0, false)`.
- `UiScreenGame` params are `{ctx}` (default `AppState.match_ctx`). The UI wiring is copied from tests/ui/ui_input_glue.gd plus placement/targeting routing through `presenter.world_press/world_motion`. The Esc chain is cancel modes, then disarm, then a game menu built from UiDialog with Resume, Surrender and Leave (pauses the local match). `_release_refs()` disconnects every signal on the screen's model objects so the presenter cycle is freed with the screen.
- `UiScreenSplash` is a thin subclass of `AppSplash`, the visual implementation. `UiFormatLite` (credits, clock, hash8) stands in for the not-yet-existing UiFormat/UiText, and text is plain English strings. `UiMapNames` is the minimal syllable table, `UiMapPreview` and `UiFactionBadge` (draws the `style.emblems` op lists) are new controls. `UiScreenKit` holds shared builders.
- The lobby row height is a local 44 px (`UiLobbySlotRow.ROW_H`) instead of `UiMetrics.LOBBY_ROW_H` = 48 so eight rows plus the briefing fit at 1080 and 720 px; UiMetrics is untouched.
- `UiLobbyState.Err` = `NetLobby.StartError` codes; `to_config(rng)` produces the net.md 7.1 dictionary (players get compact pids, None-team slots get unique team ids 5+slot, Brutal AI has handicap 120, `net.speed_pct` carries the game speed). The last setup is saved to `user://last_skirmish.json` (`UiLobbyState.save_path` is overridable for tests).
- `AppAiHook.factory` is `Callable(pid, level, style, seed) -> Callable(world)`. The bot is found by the global class name SimBot (the src->tests reference is forbidden by lint L005), and thinkers are lambdas because a Callable does not keep a RefCounted alive. A bot-driven human turns off the presenter's auto placement.
- The lobby and the loading screen rebuild the theme with the local faction's skin; the main menu uses the featured faction's.

**Tests:** HEADLESS BOOT (acceptance): `tools/gd run res://src/app/boot.tscn --allow-errors -- --autostart=match --ticks=1200 --speed=0 --bots --fresh-settings --no-audio`. Prints `APP_READY mode=match data_hash=41D8D123`, `APPTEST tick=.. chain=.. checksum=..` every 200 ticks, `APPTEST_END reason=ticks tick=1200`, and exits 0. Two runs with the same flags gave identical checksum lines. An 8-player 192-cell match with 8 bots ran 1500 ticks cleanly. Flags are `--autostart=match --ticks=N --speed=0` (0 = unpaced) `--bots[=all] --players=N --humans=H --rosters=a,b --map-seed=S --map-size=N --family=F --ai-level=L --fog=0|1 --with-ui --pause-at=N --preview=tags --drive --drive-tick=N --surrender-at=N --timeout-s=S --quit-on-end`. `--allow-errors` is only needed because of the AppBoot `UiKeymap.load_from` engine error (first request below).

UNIT TESTS
- `tools/gd test test_app_match -q`: 15 passed, 139 checks, exit 0. Covers the lobby model, job phases, session pacing and determinism, commands, pause, surrender, AI hook and bots.
- `tools/gd test test_ui_screens_menu -q`: 9 passed, 74 checks, exit 0. Covers menu, lobby, loading, credits, fatal, dialogs, badge, the game screen sending commands thr

**Known gaps:**
- First-run dialog, LAN/host/client lobby roles (chat, ready, kick, spectators, lobby name/password), Save preset (presets can be loaded from a dropdown), Field Manual clicks from the briefing, and replays are not implemented. The LAN, Replays and Options menu buttons are disabled until UiScreenLanBrowser, UiScreenReplays and UiScreenOptions exist (they enable themselves when the classes appear).
- The end screen is still the UI2 placeholder. The game screen shows the banner and passes `{summary}`; observer/replay HUD and the score are not built.
- Audio, notifier and captions are not wired (UiAudioPortNull), no cursors are registered, and there is no options access from the Esc menu.
- `--script-cmds` and `--autostart=replay` of AppTestBoot are not implemented. `--headless-match` is not a flag: use `--autostart=match`.
- View gaps show in the screenshots: structures render as magenta placeholder boxes, no baked icons (cards use the glyph fallback), no placement ghost/range rings/rally lines, and the UI-side minimap shows terrain plus dots and fog but not deposit depletion.
- The showcase is a static tableau with the camera orbiting. It is rebuilt on each menu visit (about 1 s hitch behind a black curtain) and is not cached. Cancelling a load while the view stage is mid-build hides the stage and frees it when its build finishes.
- The loading screen's per-player bars for AI players mirror the local bar (single-player has no separate loads).
- Cross-OS: only the sim and match tests were run on Linux amd64/arm64 via `gd linux test`; no Windows run.

**Cross-module requests:**
- AppBoot (UI2): `_step_services` calls `_call_static_optional(&"UiKeymap", &"load_from", [store])`, but `load_from` is an instance method, so every boot prints `Can't call non-static function 'load_from'`. That makes `gd run` exit 3 and fails `test_ui_boot::test_boot_timeline_within_budgets`. Fix: `UiKeymap.instance().load_from(store)`. Also add a `--headless-match` alias for `--autostart=match` if that spelling is wanted.
- UI1 (`UiStyleBox`): a fill narrower than the chamfer gives a degenerate polygon and logs `canvas_item_add_polygon: triangulation failed` on the Linux dummy renderer; the loading screen works around it by showing values below 4 percent as empty.
- UI4 (`UiHudPresenter`/HUD): the presenter connects lambdas capturing itself to selection, groups and placement, so it is never freed; `test_ui_hud` leaks at exit. It needs a `release()` that disconnects; UiScreenGame currently disconnects everything from outside.
- UI3 (`UiNetPortSession`): it calls `local_pid()`, `tick()`, `can_submit()`, `is_observer()` and `pending_count()` by name, which the net spec's NetSession does not expose as methods (`local_pid` is a variable). `AppNetPortLocal` is the workaround.
- Net module: `AppLocalSession` shows the surface `AppNet` needs (phase signals, `poll()`, `tick_alpha()`, `world()`, `submit_command()`, `config()`); an `AppNet` autoload and `AppEvents` should replace `AppSessionDriver` and the inline event handling in `UiScreenGame._process`, and `AppMatch.begin_build` is the `opts.world_builder`.
- AI module: install `AppAiHook.factory` from `AiFactory.make`.
- View module: `ViewWorld` should absorb what `AppViewStage` composes (mood, atmosphere, water, decor, fog feed, minimap bake) and provide structure recipes, the icon service, the placement ghost and `showcase()`; `AppViewStage` then shrinks to a thin wrapper.
- project.godot: nothing required now. When AppNet/AppAudio arrive they register there (UI2/net owner).

# === Wave 4a net+AI (wf_1d6dcbac-b77) ===

## NETA:wire+transports

**Summary:** NET-1 (wire layer) and NET-2 (transports) are done, and 77 net tests pass on macOS and in the Debian container. The checks were `tools/gd check src/net tests/net --strict` (0 warnings) and `tools/gd test test_net_` on both.
- All spec 10.2 vectors are byte-exact: FNV, varint/zvarint, mix32/lobby_rand and the shuffle, and the goldens for JOIN_REQUEST, TURN_INPUT, both TURN_BUNDLEs, PING, PONG, CHECKSUM_REPORT and START. The S0 input chain also matches (A6C168F3 after 10 turns).
- Fuzz runs 2000 random plus 2000 mutated buffers per decoder with zero engine errors, and every accepted buffer re-encodes to identical bytes. Truncation at every length is rejected.
- Real ENet on 127.0.0.1 passes: connect data, a 4 MB message intact and ordered, KICKED followed by a graceful disconnect with code 4242 in order, quiet port scan, connect timeout at 6.0-6.5 s, dual-stack (IPv4, ::1, localhost).
- Loopback hub, the fault decorator (latency, jitter, loss, dup, reorder, freeze, bandwidth, cut, corrupt hook), NetPeerStats and NetRateLimiter pass their tests. Same seed gives an identical delivery schedule.
- The NetLobbyState/NetPlayerSlot adapters in NetLobbyCodec (`encode_snapshot`, `decode_snapshot`, `state_to_dict`, `dict_to_state`) are untested against the real classes, because NET-5 and NET-10 files did not exist yet.

**Deviations (downstream code relies on these):**
- MESSAGE SPLIT. NetCodec has JOIN_REQUEST/REJECT/ACCEPT, DATA_DIFF, TURN_INPUT, TURN_BUNDLE, PING, PONG, STALL_INFO, PAUSE_REQUEST, RESUME, CHECKSUM_REPORT, DESYNC_NOTICE, PARTS_REQUEST/REPORT, MATCH_END and the CATCHUP_* messages. NetLobbyCodec has LOBBY_SNAPSHOT, LOBBY_ACTION, CHAT, LEAVE, KICKED, MAP_PING, LAUNCH_COUNTDOWN/ABORT/CONFIG, LOAD_PROGRESS/STATUS/DONE/FAILED, START and RETURN_TO_LOBBY. All are named encode_<msg>/decode_<msg>. Two use split names for the two directions: encode_chat_c2h/h2c and encode_map_ping_c2h/h2c.
- DECODED KEYS. Keys are the spec's snake_case field names. Array-valued fields are: `entries` = Array of PackedInt32Array for STALL_INFO [pid, reason, wait_ms] and LOAD_STATUS [pid, pct]; `entries` = Array of PackedInt64Array [pid, checksum, chain] for DESYNC_NOTICE; `parts` = PackedInt64Array for PARTS_*; `lines` = PackedStringArray for DATA_DIFF. Other decoded fields: PONG `hitch` is bool, PAUSE_REQUEST `want_paused` is bool, DATA_DIFF has `wants_files`. JOIN_REQUEST decodes to {proto_version, flags, spectator, sim_version, data_hash, client_nonce, game_version, name} plus optional pw_proof (PackedByteArray 32), data_format + tables (Dictionary), files (Dictionary); its encoder derives flag bits 1-3 from key presence, and duplicate table names or unknown flag bits are rejected. RETURN_TO_LOBBY (field-less) decodes to {"type": 0x17} so a valid decode is non-empty.
- CATCHUP_CHUNK. encode_catchup_chunk takes {first_turn, turn_count, raw} and deflates the body itself. decode_catchup_chunk returns {first_turn, turn_count, raw} with raw already inflated and its size verified.
- STRICTER CODEC RULES (all canonical). Command ints[0] must be 0..255 in TURN_INPUT and TURN_BUNDLE, otherwise decode fails and encode returns empty. Ctrl records are range-checked: INPUT_DELAY d 1..8; PLAYER_STATUS pid 0..7, status 0..7, aux 0..255; SPEED 50..200; MATCH_END reason 0..4; PAUSE by_pid 0..7 or 255. TURN_INPUT accepts 0..64 commands and TURN_BUNDLE groups need 1..64. Enum-like fields are range-checked on decode. Extra helpers: NetCodec.open(bytes, type) -> NetReader or null, and prefix peeks peek_join_request (returns {proto_version}), peek_join_reject (returns {host_proto_version, reason}) and peek_turn_input_header (returns {turn, exec_turn}), which lets the host substitute an empty input.
- NetBundle. build() copies its inputs. Extra members: hash32(), commands_of(pid), is_encodable(). wire_bytes() returns EMPTY for content that violates a limit; encode_turn_core returns EMPTY likewise. core_bytes() = u32 turn | u8 group_count | groups (no flags), and decode_bundle primes the caches from the input.
- LOBBY SNAPSHOT. encode_snapshot_dict / decode_snapshot_dict(bytes, opts) use a plain Dictionary whose keys mirror NetLobbyState/NetPlayerSlot (map_size in cells, auto_drop_ms, slot start -1 = random, handicap_pct). encode_snapshot(state) and decode_snapshot(bytes, opts) are duck-typed adapters (state_to_dict / dict_to_state); decode_snapshot finds NetLobbyState and NetPlayerSlot via ProjectSettings.get_global_class_list() and returns null if they do not exist yet. Range checks use opts.color_count / ai_level_count / ai_style_count when opts is an Object, otherwise 256. Slot dictionaries also carry `index`. LOBBY_ACTION decode gives {op, roster|team|color|start|ready|name|slot}; SET_START 255 maps to -1.
- NetProtocol ADDITIONS. CONNECT_MAGIC 0x4D460000, connect_data(), has_connect_magic(); MSG_TABLE with msg_max_bytes / msg_channel / msg_dir / msg_rate_class / msg_name / is_known_msg (DIR_* and RATE_* constants); looks_like_zlib(bytes, from); truncate_utf8; utf8_size; TICK_US, TURN_MS, NAME_MAX_BYTES 48, MAX_CATCHUP_CHUNK, MAX_CATCHUP_RAW. TIMEOUTS_LOBBY/GAME/LOADING are PackedInt32Array [limit, min_ms, max_ms]. sanitize_name also removes bidi and zero-width characters and caps to 48 UTF-8 bytes so it always fits the wire field. describe_reject(reason, info) reads info keys host_/local_ game_version, proto_version, sim_version, data_hash, detail; hashes print as %08X. parse_address returns {ok, host, port, error}.
- NetWriter/NetReader ADDITIONS. NetWriter has i32() (masking); NetReader has i32() and done() (ok and nothing left). Reader str_ validates UTF-8 with NetProtocol.is_valid_utf8 before the engine decoder.
- TRANSPORT SEMANTICS. disconnect_peer(graceful=true): the remote gets its queued packets then DISCONNECTED(code), and the local side later gets DISCONNECTED(0). disconnect_peer(graceful=false): the remote gets DISCONNECTED(code) at once and there is NO local event. close() on ENet sends peer_disconnect_now(0) to connected peers, flushes, and destroys the host. send() returns ERR_INVALID_PARAMETER for channel outside 0..2 or empty data, and ERR_UNAVAILABLE for an unknown or not-yet-connected peer. peer_stats() never returns null (zeroed for gone peers) and never queries a non-connected ENet peer. take_events() and the event queue live in the NetTransport base class; other base methods push_error("NOT IMPLEMENTED") as specified.
- NetTransportEnet. Constructor is `NetTransportEnet.new(clock: NetClock = null)` (null = real clock; the 6 s connect timeout uses it). Default peer timeouts are TIMEOUTS_LOBBY until set_timeouts() is called. A failed connect (timeout, resolve failure) is reported as DISCONNECTED(peer 1, code 0); there is no CONNECTED before it. One listen() per transport instance (a second call returns ERR_ALREADY_IN_USE). Hostnames are resolved asynchronously (5 s limit) inside the 6 s deadline.
- NetLoopbackHub / NetTransportLoopback. `hub.endpoint(key)` returns the endpoint labelled `key` (hub-local label, created on first use). `hub.connect_endpoints(client, host, connect_data=0) -> int` links two endpoints directly (client sees host as peer 1) and returns the host-side peer id. Endpoints also support the normal listen/connect_to path via the hub port registry (`hub.reserve_port()` occupies ports for scan tests). Zero latency: messages sit in the remote inbox until that endpoint's poll() or `hub.deliver_all()`. A connect to a missing or full listener reports DISCONNECTED after `hub.connect_fail_ms` (default 6000) on the hub clock (manual by default, `hub.clock`). Endpoints also have `address` (default "127.0.0.1", used as CONNECTED.address). The hub holds endpoints strongly while endpoints hold the hub and each other weakly (no reference cycles), so the caller MUST keep the hub in a variable.
- NetTransportFault. It models the SEND side: a sent message gets a delivery time and is handed to the inner transport in poll() once due, so the inner transport's own delivery adds at most one poll interval; wrap the sending endpoint(s). Ordered mode implements the spec's head-of-line rule (so with jitter the mean latency exceeds the profile's; unordered mode has exact mean). During a freeze poll() neither services the inner transport nor releases, and take_events() returns empty; sends during a freeze leave at freeze end plus latency. Periodic freeze windows are [k*freeze_every_ms, +freeze_ms) for k>=1 measured from wrap time. graceful disconnect_peer is scheduled after the peer's last delivery (ordering preserved); non-graceful is immediate and purges queued items. disconnect_after_ms cuts all peers (hard, plus local DISCONNECTED events) and makes send() return ERR_UNAVAILABLE. stats() keys: sent, delayed, dropped, retransmitted, duplicated, reordered, corrupted, held, delivered, max_delay_ms. Extras: pending_count(), record_schedule / schedule (delivery times in us, for reproducibility tests), inner_transport(). corrupt_next flips the last byte of the next message to that peer/channel.
- NetFaultProfile. preset(name) returns null for an unknown name; extras: PRESET_NAMES, from_dict({preset?, name?, <field>...}) for profiles.json entries, to_dict(), duplicate_profile(), effective_rto_ms().
- NetRateLimiter. Buckets are integer micro-tokens (no float drift). The tables are const RATE_PER_S / BURST (PackedInt32Array indexed by Class); unknown class falls back to MISC. add_violation/score decay 1 point per VIOLATION_DECAY_MS as floats, so a kick check should use >= 12 with equal timestamps (4 x weight 3 at the same instant = exactly 12).
- Corrupt DEFLATE streams. `inflate_config` and `decode_catchup_chunk` pre-check the zlib header and sizes, so only a valid-looking but corrupt stream reaches the engine inflater. That case, and an over-limit inflate (zip bomb), logs exactly ONE engine error "Decompression failed". Callers/tests that feed such data must declare expect_errors(1), as the spec allows. Header-only mutation fuzz of these two decoders is error-free.

**Tests:** `tools/gd check src/net tests/net --strict`: lint 0, scripts 0 errors, 0 warnings. `tools/gd test test_net_` (macOS): 77 passed, 0 failed, 0 errors (10549 checks, about 9 s). `tools/gd linux test test_net_` (Debian container): 77 passed (10549 checks). Files: test_net_protocol (12 tests), test_net_codec (23), test_net_transport_loopback (17, includes NetClock, NetPeerStats, NetRateLimiter, NetFaultProfile), test_net_transport_enet (9, real sockets), test_net_fault (14). Expected engine errors, all declared with expect_errors: the zip-bomb inflate (1) and NetTransport base-class NOT IMPLEMENTED calls (5).

**Known gaps:**
- NetLobbyCodec.encode_snapshot/decode_snapshot (the NetLobbyState adapters) are untested against the real NetLobbyState/NetPlayerSlot, which did not exist yet; the Dictionary form is fully tested. NET-5/NET-6 should add one round-trip test once the classes land.
- ENet dead-peer detection timing (E5, 4.8-6.5 s) is not tested; set_timeouts is only smoke-tested. IPv6 is tested only if ::1 works (it did on both macOS and the Linux container).
- The 50-run cross-OS determinism script (xplat_determinism.py) was not needed for this task; codec goldens are byte-exact on macOS and linux/amd64 (arm64 not run).
- Sender-side fault model means an inbound-only wrapped endpoint gets no latency; wrap the sender (documented).
- Catch-up (spectator) messages are wire-layout only, no consumer; NetDiscovery datagram codec is NET-7 (not part of this task).

**Cross-module requests:**


## AIA:foundation+knowledge+data

**Summary:** AI-01/02/03 lean foundation is in and green: 34 files in game/src/ai, 5 JSON files in game/data/balance/ai, 15 test files in game/tests/ai (including AiMockWorldView). An AiController with a trivial op plays 2000 real ticks in a SimMatchKit match as Hard, issuing valid commands. The sim builds and trains from them. Average cost is 7 wu/tick against the profile's 500, about 11 us/tick against the 1.5 ms target. The purity lint passes: no world writes, no clock or float trig, only AiWorldView touches Sim* classes, only AiCommandBuilder uses SimCmd. Only Brutal gets fog-piercing reads and the labelled +20 percent handicap (ai.cheats.brutal). All 32 rosters bootstrap with their essential roles. Modules plug into the fixed 15-slot scheduler through AiController.register(slot, module), where a module has setup(ctx), step(ctx, budget) and state_hash(). Slot 0 (event ingest) and slot 1 (knowledge upkeep) are pre-registered. Not built: AiCond, AiDataValidator, AiAvoidZones, the AiDebug trace ring, AiPlacer, AiBrain and every module from AI-04 on.

**Deviations (downstream code relies on these):**
- AiContext.brain is typed RefCounted, not AiBrain, because AiBrain does not exist yet. The AI-06 owner should retype it. AiContext also gained store, telemetry, roster_idx, res (AiRoleResolver), tech (AiTechGraph), pid, and helpers unit_profile(def), struct_profile(def), unit_profile_of(p, def), struct_profile_of(p, def), tune(path, default).
- AiCommandBuilder additions and changes. begin_think(dt, tick = -1) takes an optional world tick. build_place(struct_def, cx, cy, rot = 0) has an extra rot argument. force_fire(units, x, y, count = 0, target_eid = 0) has an extra target argument (0 = ground point). harvest(units, deposit_id, x, y) needs the field centre, because the sim's HARVEST command resolves the field from the point. use_power(power_def, x, y, angle, x2, y2) ignores x2 and y2, since the sim's USE_POWER carries one point. set_mode sends def = -1. New methods: tokens(), now(), recent(), state_hash(), is_blocked(intent, key, tick), note_effect(intent, key), observe_lag(ticks), and static key2 and cell_key. Blacklist keys: BUILD_START struct_def; BUILD_PLACE key2(def, key2(cx, cy)); TRAIN key2(producer, def); RESEARCH res_def; unit orders use the target eid or cell_key(x, y). note_no_effect: first strike backs off 60 ticks, later strikes blacklist for 1200.
- AiBudget gained a `used` counter, which the scheduler uses to charge each slot.
- AiScheduler.run(tick, dt, ctx, budget) gives each due slot its own sub-budget of quota x min(4, ceil(dt/period)). A late slot runs first. After 3 skipped thinks a slot runs on its reserved quota even into debt. register(slot, module) is the plug-in point. Slot enum: INGEST 0, KB 1, WATCHDOG 2, DISPERSAL 3, ECONOMY 4, BUILD 5, TECH 6, PRODUCTION 7, STRATEGY 8, DEFENSE 9, ATTACK 10, OPS 11, POWERS 12, MICRO 13, SUPPORT 14.
- AiWorldView. The constructor is new(me, omniscient). The clock accessor is think_dt(), because dt() shadowed the begin_think parameter. can_place(def, cx, cy, rot = 0) takes an optional rotation. can_build reports NO_CREDITS when credits are below cost/4, a heuristic for progressive payment. Extra reads beyond the spec: game_data, player_slots, roster_id_of, unit_cost/ticks and struct_cost/ticks/power, unit_cap_room, struct_counts, struct_count, construction_queue, has_hq, producer_ids, producer_kind, refinery_ids, collector_ids, buildable_units/structures, researchable, e_kind, e_layer, deposit_klass/radius/cap/harvesters/max_harvesters, start_cell_x/y, income_per_minute, power_shortage, power_slot.
- Deposit id in AiWorldView is the economy field index 0..n-1 (deposit_x/y are the field centre), not a neutral entity id. neutral_ids returns only the capturable or garrisonable neutral entities.
- AiWorldView reads that are stubbed until the sim provides them: strategic_warnings() returns 0 (the strategic system is a stub), e_mode() returns 0 (no public mode reader), e_cargo() counts entities whose container_id matches (the cargo component is a stub). sw_status never reports WARNING.
- AiRouteGraph takes an AiDataStore, not a map. Bind it with ensure_map(view) or bind_map(w, h, passable_callable). route() returns the number of waypoints, and out holds 2 ints each. New APIs: make_job() returning an AiRouteJob (a resumable AiJob), connected(), needs_water(), snap_open(), fork(), and disjoint_route(..., avoid = waypoints of the first route). chokes_on(route, out, move_class = TRACKED) has an extra argument. Grids are built lazily per move class from at most 16 sampled cells per 8x8 block, so they are a snapshot of the map at first use. AiKnowledge.setup gives each AI a forked handle with its own threat map and clock over the shared immutable grids.
- AiCluster.best_circle(ctx, r, mode, k, out, budget = null, enemy_mask = 0xFF) and score_shape(ctx, shape, x, y, angle, mode = MODE_ALL, enemy_mask = 0xFF) take extra arguments. Shapes are Arrays of part dictionaries with types c (circle), a (annulus) and r (rectangle); mode -1 reuses the last collect(). Inputs are ghosts (value x hp%) and seen enemy units (unit cost x hp/hp_max). best_circle returns the number of circles found.
- AiEntityTable gained a `refreshed` and a `kind` column. AiKnowledge exposes own, enemy_units, ghosts, threat, profiles[8], sites, route, cluster, events (Array[PackedInt32Array] of records delivered this think), now, camo, attacked_by_tick, enemy_starts/enemy_pids, primary, and the income EMA fields (income_ema_q8, income_per_min, collectors_alive). Event record layout is [Ev code, a, b, c, d, e] with AiTypes.Ev codes. Enemy unit rows store the unit's roster cost in `paid`, not the real paid_cost. That value feeds the seen_value and army-value estimates.
- AiStrength.ratio_q8(att, def, home_adv_pct = 0, noise_q8 = 0) takes the home advantage and noise as arguments, so the caller passes the tuning value (10). AiStrengthGroup gained arty_dps_x100, arty_hp, count and an add(profile, n, cur_hp, val) helper. Static noise_q8(tick, target_key, est_noise_pct, epoch = 200) provides the stable estimate noise.
- AiPersonality.build(store, roster_id, lobby_style, diff, rng, override) consumes rng draws (0) to (3) of ai.md 5.14.3. Jittered fields are aggression, tech, economy, defense_pct, harass_pct, air, naval, siege, infantry, micro and sw_priority. Draw (4), the opener steps, and (5), the superweapon timing, are left to AiBuildPlanner and AiSuperweapon. A wildcard style resolves to style_mode 0..2 plus an attack style among PUSH..LANDING. It always allows LANDING, even on a map with no water.
- Data files. Roster ids are the real ones (roster.olm.saudi_arabia, roster.nec.alpine_brotherhood, ...). ai_personality.json holds {default, rosters{}} with the 32 base rows of ai.md 5.14.2; per-roster micro is 50 for everyone. ai_roles.json uses the schema {roles: {NAME: {all, none, tier_max, pick, ids}}, unit_roles, unit_handlers}. ai_tuning.json adds ingest.*, route.* and clusters.* keys. ai_difficulty.json gained cheats_text, the English text for Brutal's label. AiDataStore reads a fixed file list: 4 required files plus optional ai_composition.json, ai_powers.json and ai_faction_<code>.json, which are kept raw in `extra`. AI-10 must keep these names and shapes or update AiDataStore.
- AiRoleResolver.classify_structure derives StructKind from queue kind, power, tags and the requires chain, with no structure ids. AiTechGraph.missing(def, have, pending) treats the roster HQ as owned. AiUnitProfile.kiter is not computed (only the optional unit_roles.KITER list feeds the role bit).
- AiThinker.think reads the tick with world.get("tick") and does nothing when dt <= 0. The thinker keeps no reference to the factory, because that reference caused a RefCounted cycle and leaked objects at exit. AiFactory gained live_count(), fields debug_level, perf, personality_overrides and tuning_override, and rebalances the wu governor to 2400/n per AI on make and release. AiFactory.debug_frame returns a minimal panel-only AiDebugFrame at debug level 2.

**Tests:** tools/gd check src/ai tests/ai --strict: 0 lint violations, 0 script errors, 0 warnings. tools/gd test _ai_ -q: 76 tests, 76 passed, 4191 checks, on macOS arm64 and in tools/gd linux (amd64). Full suite tools/gd test -q: 1306 tests, 1305 passed, 1 error (ui/test_ui_boot.gd::test_boot_timeline_within_budgets, a UI test that fails independently of the AI code). Coverage: rng vectors (mix32, the six-value streams, thinker_seed, hash_str), budget debt/no-carry/cap, scheduler periods/due arithmetic/starvation guard and average wu over dt 1..50 at all 4 levels, command builder (last order wins, 48/48/4 chunking, APM bucket and 30 percent reserve, class order, 64-command cap, blacklist, wire types 40..47), purity lint, data store and difficulty rows (only Brutal cheats, V10 negative case), personality determinism and style effects, roles for all 32 rosters (essentials, tank tier 2 for the seven, artillery absent only for Japan, healer NAPC-only, no replaced defs), profiles of every producible def in every roster, tech graph vectors, strength vectors (296 and 581, 4096 for a harmless defender), threat decay, route graph (corridors, threat shift, disjoint route, chokes, unreachable, needs_w

**Known gaps:**
- No AiBrain, AiPlacer, AiCond, AiDataValidator (only V6/V8/V10 in AiDataStore.validate), AiAvoidZones, AiDebug trace ring or AiWatchdog. Modules from AI-04 onward are not built. No exception containment (GDScript has no try/catch); the watchdog task must add stall detection.
- The route graph is a coarse snapshot: 16 samples per 8x8 block, built on first use of a move class, not refreshed when structures change. Its first build costs a few ms, charged as budget debt.
- The enemy-composition prior is flat over the categories the roster can field (air, armor, infantry, artillery, naval, light), not the doctrine-authored prior of ai_composition.json. Camouflage sightings set only a flag on the enemy profile.
- Superweapon warnings, cargo and mode reads are stubbed in AiWorldView, as listed under deviations. power_status is an approximation from the power slot's ready_tick, prerequisite mask, credits and the player's powers_online flag. Nothing feeds the arty-fired or unseen-hit alerts from sim events; they are derived from state.
- Per-level think periods [5,3,2,1] turns are used only in the demo harness. The net owner must implement request 13.16 (AI_THINK_PERIOD_TURNS_BY_LEVEL).
- The demo's trivial op is test-local (tests/ai/test_ai_demo.gd) and is not a shippable module.

**Cross-module requests:**


## NETB:lockstep+turnhost+lobbymodel

**Summary:** NET-3, NET-4 and NET-5 are done. The sim seam (fake and REAL adapter), the lockstep runner, the host turn assembly with delay policy and AI runner, and the lobby/match-config model with lobby_options.json are all in place.
The acceptance test drives host plus 2 loopback clients over real NetTransportLoopback, NetCodec, NetTurnHost and NetLockstep, with PING/PONG feeding the delay policy. Each peer advances its own real SimWorld (built via SimMatchSetup.create_world) with SimBots as the command source. All 3 worlds stayed identical for 600 ticks and for a longer 2400-tick match.
A second real test resigns one player and drops another; the match then ends on the same tick with the same final checksum and chain on every peer.
The S0 goldens from spec 10.2 match at ticks 20..100 for checksum, chain and parts. Pre-roll turns 0..D0-1 must be included in the input chain to match.
`tools/gd test net`: 141 tests, all pass (12363 checks). `check --strict` reports nothing in my files; the 19 script errors it prints are all in src/ai (another agent's work in progress).

**Deviations (downstream code relies on these):**
- PRE-ROLL: NetLockstep.start() inserts implicit empty bundles for turns 0..D0-1 into its own queue and sets _next_push = D0. NetTurnHost does NOT close them: its first bundle is turn D0 (_next_close = D0, _recv_through = D0-1, _inject_through = D0-1). on_turn_begin and the input chain DO include the pre-roll turns (empty core bytes); the S0 goldens require this. A recorder should skip turns < D0. push_bundle for turns < D0 returns DUPLICATE.
- exec_turn() = current_tick() / 2 (the turn about to begin at a boundary). The S0 harness submits a command for execution turn T once the peer has finished tick 2*(T-D-1), so it is sent at boundary T-D.
- NetLockstep extras: is_over(), current_tick(), get_speed_pct() (renamed from speed_pct() to avoid shadowing the setup parameter), pending_count(). set_speed_pct(0) = unpaced permanently: CK_SPEED records are then ignored for pacing but still delivered to on_ctrl. The final on_checksum(end_tick, checksum_now(), chain) REPLACES the periodic one when the match ends on a multiple of 20. on_stall_changed(true, ms) fires from update() calls while stalled, once the wait reaches 400 ms.
- NetTurnHost extra hooks (Callables, all optional): on_stall_info(turn, entries: Array[PackedInt32Array [pid, StallReason, wait_ms]]) with [] as the clear message; on_stall_prompt(pids); on_status(pid, status, aux) for host-local detection; on_resume(resume_turn, by_pid) (session sends RESUME and calls lockstep.resume(R) locally); on_takeover(pid) (session calls NetAiRunner.add_ai(pid, TAKEOVER_AI_LEVEL, 0)); on_delay_changed(d). Extra methods: on_bad_input(pid, turn) (decodable header, bad body: stores an empty input, returns TOO_BIG), queue_ctrl(rec), player_left(pid) (clean LEAVE: on_disconnect policy, status LEFT), stall_wait(pid) (WAIT: suppress the prompt 30 s), role_of, pid_of_peer, recv_through, pauses_used, is_pause_active, resume_pause(by_pid). request_pause(pid, false) requires pid == pauser or host.
- NetTurnHost.drop_player(pid, mode, reason): reason is a ResignReason. RESIGN mode makes the role PR_DROPPED at once, so the pid leaves the barrier. It clears the pid's buffered inputs and queues T_RESIGN first in that pid's group of the next closed bundle. It also queues CK_PLAYER_STATUS(pid, DROPPED, reason) into the same bundle. AI mode makes the role PR_AI, discards buffered inputs and queues CK_PLAYER_STATUS(pid, AI_TAKEOVER, TAKEOVER_AI_LEVEL). STALL_WAIT is routed to stall_wait. waiting() returns only pids that blocked the last close_ready() attempt; a new blocked turn restarts the wait timer.
- NetTurnHost.setup(clock, cfg): the initial delay is fixed_delay if > 0, else initial_delay, else min_delay. All cfg keys are optional. set_player after start uses recv_through = max(D0, next_close) - 1. The delay policy uses turn_ms = 10000 / speed_pct and is re-configured on set_speed().
- NetAiRunner extras: add_ai returns bool; seed_of(pid); period_of(pid); is_overdue(pid); release_all(); set_governor(bool); time_source Callable (test hook). The overdue-first ordering is internal; output groups are always ascending by pid. A thinker slower than 12 ms twice in a row has its period doubled; a Log.warn is emitted.
- NetLobbyState.spectators is an untyped Array, not Array[Dictionary]. NetLobbyCodec.dict_to_state assigns it through Object.set(), which silently refuses an untyped Array for a typed property, so the snapshot round trip lost spectators otherwise. NetLobbyState/NetPlayerSlot/NetMatchConfig take opts as RefCounted, duck-typed by property name, because NetSessionOptions (NET-10) does not exist yet.
- NetLobbyState.create_default opens every slot below layout_players (slot 0 = host HUMAN, or OPEN when opts.dedicated); slots >= layout_players are CLOSED. create_skirmish: slot 0 HUMAN, slot 1 AI (medium level, roster random, ready), the rest CLOSED, layout_players 4 and allow_spectators false. The AI slot's handicap comes from opts.ai_default_handicap when provided.
- NetMatchConfig.from_lobby gained an optional 5th parameter roster_ids: PackedStringArray. It falls back to the static NetMatchConfig.default_roster_ids (the app sets it from GameData). versions may carry an optional match_id (16 hex); otherwise the id is derived deterministically from seed and now_unix (no RNG or clock). net.input_delay starts at D_MIN_LAN; the session must overwrite it (cfg.net.input_delay) for LOCAL or a fixed delay before canonical_json. rules holds only the 8 rules_schema keys; the other SimMatchRules fields keep sim defaults. Extra statics: options(), rules_schema(), default_rules(), token_candidates(), is_random_token(), is_roster_ok(), shuffle_groups(), parse(json_text) (returns {} on failure without engine errors).
- NetMatchConfig.normalize requires ALL keys of each object (exact key sets). Bool-typed rules and net.allow_spectators must be JSON booleans; integer fields accept int or integral float. It also requires name == sanitize_name(name), handicap % 5 == 0, team in 1..4 or exactly 8 + pid, at most layout_players players, unique human peers, and empty map.params. validate() also rejects random tokens that were not resolved.
- NetSimAdapterFake.inject_divergence(at_tick, part) flips bit 0 of part (0 = cmds word, 1 = clock word) when the tick counter reaches at_tick, before that tick's snapshot. end_tick is a public var. NetSimAdapterWorld.submit_command ignores an empty or over-1024 array (counted in malformed_count) and forwards everything else to SimWorld.submit_raw. map_hash() forwards to world.map_hash().
- NetWorldJob is a plain class with sync(builder) as the only shipped constructor. Sliceable jobs subclass it and override step, progress_pct, error and take_adapter.

**Tests:** ["`tools/gd test net -q`: 170 files, 141 tests, 141 passed, 0 failed, 0 errors (12363 checks); includes NET-1/2 tests already there.", "`tools/gd test test_net_lockstep_unit`: fake adapter, fill-up table (E=10..15 with INPUT_DELAY 3/2), barrier stall at tick 6, caps (8 ticks/poll, 4-tick accumulator), unpaced, S0 goldens on 2 peers, pause and quick-toggle watermark, 200 % speed, match-over final checksum, WorldJob, and 3-peer random-script equality.", "`tools/gd test test_net_turn_host` (16 tests: every 5.5.5 row, barrier, 16-per-call cap, groups/T_RESIGN/AI/ctrl, drop RESIGN/AI, waiting reasons, stall info/prompt 8 s/auto-drop 60 s and DISCONNECTED 5 s, pause budget/permissions/expiry, delay policy integration), `test_net_delay_policy` (a)-(g), `test_net_ai_runner` (cadence, per-level periods, sanitising, seeds, governor, slow thinker), `test_net_match_config` (§7.1 round trip, ~60 rejects, RNG shuffle vector [7,3,2,0,1,6,5,4], random-token resolution reproducible and independent of edit order, lobby model, lobby_options.json, snapshot codec round trip with the real model).", "`tools/gd test test_net_adapter_world`: adapter forwarding incl. T_RESIGN; host + 2 loopback clients, rea

**Known gaps:**
- Not run cross-OS (the task did not require xplat_determinism); the adapter test uses only integer sim state and the manual clock, so it should be portable.
- The real-adapter test uses 3 human seats driven by SimBot; AI takeover through NetAiRunner is covered against the fake adapter only. No NetSession/NetLaunch glue exists yet, so the session-level wiring (poll order, STALL_INFO/RESUME messages, recorder skipping pre-roll turns) is left to NET-10.
- NetTurnHost.on_input never scores violations; the caller must apply the scores from the spec table. It also does not send any message: the session wires the on_* hooks.
- The lobby permission/normalisation rules of 5.3.2/5.3.3 (swaps, ready reset, unique names) belong to NetLobby (NET-6) and are not in NetLobbyState.
- SimMatchRules fields outside the 8 lobby rule keys (start_mode, neutral_structures, victory, end_when_no_humans, allow_debug) cannot be set through MatchConfig.

**Cross-module requests:**
- NET-10 (session): construct NetLockstep with the D0 chosen as in spec 5.4, set net.input_delay in the config before canonical_json for LOCAL (D_MIN_LOCAL) or a fixed delay, wire NetTurnHost.on_takeover to NetAiRunner.add_ai(pid, NetProtocol.TAKEOVER_AI_LEVEL, 0), on_resume to the RESUME message plus lockstep.resume(R), on_stall_info/on_stall_prompt to STALL_INFO and the stall_prompt signal, and call host.note_injection_through(target) from lockstep.on_boundary after NetAiRunner.produce. Real world_builder callables must create worlds through SimMatchSetup.create_world (see tests/net/test_net_adapter_world.gd, SimMatchKit.make_match).
- NET-6 (lobby): NetLobbyState.spectators is an untyped Array; NetMatchConfig.default_roster_ids (or the roster_ids argument) must be set from GameData.rosters ids before from_lobby resolves random tokens.
- App / sim: add SimConfig.SIM_VERSION (spec XR-8) so NetSessionOptions.sim_version and versions.sim can be filled; NetMatchConfig.validate reads opts.sim_version by property name.
- AI module: provide AiFactory.think_period_turns(level) ([5,3,2,1]), level_handicap_pct(level) and release(pid) as callables for NetAiRunner.setup / NetSessionOptions; the runner passes thinker.call(world, out: Array) and expects PackedInt32Array commands.
- NET-8/9 (replay, desync): use NetSimAdapterFake.inject_divergence(at_tick, part) semantics (flip applied when the tick counter reaches at_tick, before the snapshot) and NetLockstepKit (tests/support/net_lockstep_kit.gd) as the zero-latency relay rig.

## NETC:lobby+discovery+desync+session

**Summary:** NETC done. NetSession is the single facade for HOST, CLIENT and LOCAL (LOCAL = same launch/turn-host/lockstep/AI-runner path, no transport). NetLobby covers the lobby protocol; NetLaunch runs countdown, config, world job, LOAD_DONE compare and START. NetDiscovery does UDP announce/browse with breaker and expiry. NetDesync adds classification, packages and retention; NetSelfTest.run_double compares a LOCAL run against a replay-driven world. NetHostGate enforces the message matrix and violation scoring (kick at 12). NetSession.stats() and the full signal/enum set follow the spec. Cross-OS: xplat_net_local.gd (LOCAL real-sim bot match, 125 hashes) is identical on macOS arm64, linux amd64 and linux arm64. Public API is listed as the 'API' items under deviations.

**Deviations (downstream code relies on these):**
- API (NetSession, what AppNet drives). Construct with NetSession.host(opts, lobby_name="") / join(opts, address, port=27615) / local(opts, lobby_state: NetLobbyState) / local_from_config(opts, config) -> NetSession or null. On null read the static NetSession.last_create_error. Also NetSession.create_browser(opts) -> NetDiscovery for the LAN list: call start_browse(), poll() each frame, entries(). Options are duplicated by the session. Build them with NetSessionOptions.from_game_data(data) and then set world_builder (Callable(config) -> NetWorldJob whose take_adapter() returns a NetSimAdapterWorld built through SimMatchSetup.create_world), ai_factory, map_validator (e.g. MapGenerator.validate_params) and transport_factory. Call poll() -> int once per frame BEFORE view/ui. Also tick_alpha() -> float and shutdown() (idempotent).
- API enums. NetSession.Role {NONE 0, HOST 1, CLIENT 2, LOCAL 3}. NetSession.Phase {IDLE 0, CONNECTING 1, LOBBY 2, COUNTDOWN 3, LOADING 4, WAIT_START 5, PLAYING 6, PAUSED 7, ENDED 8, DESYNCED 9, DISCONNECTED 10}. Illegal phase transitions are ignored and raise net_error(TRANSPORT). Other enums are NetProtocol.RejectReason, KickReason, AbortReason, PlayerNetStatus, MatchEndReason, NetErrorCode, PauseError, StallAction, DesyncKind and NetLobby.Result/StartError.
- API vars. role, phase, lobby (NetLobby, null for local_from_config), discovery (host only), local_peer_id (host and LOCAL = 1, client from JOIN_ACCEPT), local_pid (-1 for a dedicated host, before launch, or an all-AI LOCAL match). local_pid is a VARIABLE per spec, so UiNetPortSession's local_pid() calls need an adapter. Test/tool hooks: checksum_observer(tick, checksum, chain), turn_observer(turn, bundle), replay_bytes_provider() -> PackedByteArray.
- API methods. world(), adapter(), config() (deep copy), command_log() -> SimCommandLog (every executed command at tick = 2*turn, checkpoints every 20 ticks; replay with SimCommandLog.play(fresh_world, ticks)), submit_command(ints) -> bool, surrender(), request_pause(bool) -> 0 or PauseError, host_set_speed(pct) -> bool (HOST and LOCAL), host_resolve_stall(pid, StallAction), host_return_to_lobby() (ENDED/DESYNCED), leave(), send_chat(text, team_only=false), send_map_ping(x, y), player_status(pid), waiting_list(), is_paused(), stats() (spec 4.7 keys), replay_path() -> "", advertised_addresses(), violation_total(), peer_info(peer_id), peer_count(), log_lines().
- API signals. phase_changed(phase, previous), lobby_changed(), chat_received(channel, from_pid, from_name, text) (from_pid 255 = system), countdown_changed(seconds_left) (0 = aborted), join_rejected(reason, info), kicked(reason, detail), load_progress(pids, percents), launch_aborted(reason, detail), match_started(), stall_changed(waiting), stall_prompt(pids), pause_changed(paused, by_pid), player_status_changed(pid, status), speed_changed(pct), input_delay_changed(turns), match_ended({reason, sim_reason, winner_team, final_tick, final_checksum}), desync_detected(report §7.5), net_error(code, text), map_ping(from_pid, x, y). join_rejected info has both spec keys (host_version, host_proto, host_sim, host_data_hash, my_*, session_id, detail) and the NetProtocol.describe_reject keys (host_game_version, host_proto_version, ..., local_*), plus diff_lines (PackedStringArray) and text. A stall entry of the client for 'the host is silent' is {pid: -1, name: "host", reason: NETWORK, wait_ms}.
- NetLaunch differs from spec 3.9: reference() is launch_reference() (RefCounted.reference() clashes). on_message(peer_id, type, reader) is split into on_client_message(peer: NetPeerInfo, type, bytes, now_us) -> float (host) and on_host_message(type, bytes, session_phase) -> float (client); both return the violation weight. Extra: begin_local(), begin_local_config(cfg), prepare_config(), cancel_countdown(), on_peer_lost(), reset(), d0(), hook Callables (send, set_phase, on_aborted, on_progress, on_timeouts, on_start, on_kick, log_fn).
- Files split off NetSession to stay under the 1500-line lint limit: NetJoinClient (client join, data-diff and password retry state machine), NetHostGate (connection admission, §4.3 matrix, violation scoring), NetHostGame (turn host + AI runner wiring, host in-match handlers), NetClientGame (bundle intake, PING/PONG, stall overlay, MATCH_END check), NetDesyncFlow (DESYNC_NOTICE/PARTS sequencing). All talk to the session only through Callables.
- NetSessionOptions extras beyond spec 3.1: discovery_port, discovery_targets, allow_public_discovery, desync_dir ("user://desync"), desync_keep (10), client_nonce, join_session_id, join_as_spectator, match_seed_override, unix_time_override, record_command_log; static from_game_data(GameData); duplicate_options(). host()/join()/local() require roster_ids.size() == 32; local_from_config() does not. Data-diff and password retries call opts.transport_factory again, so it must return a fresh transport each time.
- Lobby details. Colours and start positions are unique among ACTIVE (HUMAN/AI) slots, not all non-closed slots; OPEN/CLOSED slots keep default colours. NetLobbyState.create_default is called with opts.player_name for the host slot; lobby_name only sets state.host_name. Growing layout_players opens the new slots. Closing or opening a HUMAN slot kicks its peer. Host edits of a human slot flush a snapshot immediately; other edits are coalesced to >= 100 ms. Spectators can join and switch in the lobby but are kicked when a launch starts (no spectating in matches). JOIN_ACCEPT is followed at once by a LOBBY_SNAPSHOT and the others get one too. A game_version difference produces a system chat line.
- NetDesync additions. Classification: only chain differs = INPUT_CHAIN, only checksum = SIM, both = BOTH (so a 'different command order' fault on the fake sim reports BOTH). Extra API: setup context, is_detected(), detected_tick(), detected_kind(), snapshot(tick), parts_at(tick), parts_diff(), last_good_tick() (highest stored snapshot below the detected tick), missing_parts(), build_report(), package_bases(), kind_name(). Package = <dir>/desync_<match8>_p<pid>_t<\tick>.{json,state.txt,mfreplay,checks.csv}. checks.csv has a header row 'tick,checksum,chain,<part names>'. JSON adds 'log' (session log ring), 'dir' and 'config_hash'. Retention: newest 10 packages via <dir>/desync_index.txt, foreign files are never deleted. The .mfreplay is a 'MFCLOG1' command-log-lite file (config JSON + SimCommandLog ints) unless NetSession.replay_bytes_provider is set, because NET-8 does not exist yet. The greppable DESYNC line goes through opts.log_sink (Log.error by default). The healthy clients' parts_diff is empty (they are compared to the host, and only the diverging peer differs).
- NetSelfTest.run_double uses a LOCAL NetSession with unpaced pacing, replays A's SimCommandLog into a second world (mode 'commandlog', not NetReplayPlayer) and re-derives the input chain from the rebuilt bundles. Script entries are {tick, ints} for the local player. The config's own versions.sim and data_hash are adopted for validation. Result adds 'error' and 'parts'.
- NetSession.poll(): steps 5-7 of spec 3.0 are kept, but host/LOCAL repeat 'close_ready -> update' while ticks keep coming, up to max_ticks_per_poll, so unpaced LOCAL runs are not limited to one turn per poll.
- speed_pct_override = 0 (unpaced) is for LOCAL and virtual-time tests only: on real sockets an unpaced client exceeds the 40/s TURN_INPUT bucket. The real-ENet test runs at 200 %.
- Host quit sends KICKED(HOST_LEFT) + graceful disconnect and, on manual clocks, skips the 10 x 5 ms service loop.
